To dip my toe in was to know that, unawares, I had been immersed in it all my life, up to my neck. is realization may be called the stage of ADD epiphany, the annunciation, characterized by elation, insight, enthusiasm and hope. It seemed to me that I had found the passage to those dark recesses of my mind from which chaos issues without warning, hurlingthoughts, plans, emotions and intentions in all directions. I felt I had discovered what it was that had always kept me from attaining psychological integrity: wholeness, the reconciliation and joining together of the disharmonious fragments of my mind. 试水让我意识到，不知不觉中，我已经沉浸其中，直到我的脖子。这种认识可以被称为ADD的顿悟阶段，特点是兴奋、洞察力、热情和希望。在我看来，我找到了通向我心灵黑暗角落的通道，在那里，混乱毫无征兆地出现，将思想、计划、情感和意图抛向四面八方。我觉得我发现了是什么一直让我无法获得心理完整性：完整，是我心灵不和谐的碎片的调和与结合。
I was not an undereducated teenager eager for kicks when I self-administered the Ritalin. Already in my ﬁfties, I was a successful and respected family doctor whose columns of medical opinion were praised for their thoughtfulness. I practice medicine with a high value on avoiding pharmacology unless absolutely necessary, and needless to say, I have always advised patients against self-medicating. Such striking imbalance between intellectual awareness on the one hand and emotional and behavioral self-control on the other is characteristic of people with attention deﬁcit disorder. 当我自行服用利他林时，我并不是一个渴望寻求刺激的教育程度不高的青少年。当时我已经 50 多岁了，是一名成功的、受人尊敬的家庭医生，我的医学专栏以其周到细致而广受好评。我的行医理念是，除非绝对必要，否则应尽量避免使用药物，更不用说，我总是建议病人不要自行服药。这种智力认知与情绪和行为自我控制之间的显著失衡是注意力缺陷障碍患者的特征。
Even if in many cases medications do help, the healing ADD calls for is not a process of recovery from some illness. It is a process of becoming whole—which, it so happens, is the original sense of the word healing.
即使在许多情况下，药物确实有帮助，但治愈多动症需要的不是一个从某种疾病中恢复过来的过程。这是一个成为完整的人的过程——而这恰恰是治愈这个词最初的含义。
e DSM IV deﬁnes attention deﬁcit disorder by its external features, not by its emotional meaning in the lives of individual human beings. It commits the faux pas of calling these external observations symptoms, whereas that word in medical language denotes a patient’s own felt experience. External observations, no matter how acute, are signs. A headache is a symptom. A chest sound registered by the doctor’s stethoscope is a sign. A cough is both a symptom and a sign.
DSM IV 根据其外部特征，而不是根据它在个人生活中的情感意义，将注意力缺陷多动障碍定义为一种注意缺陷障碍。它犯了将这些外部观察称为症状的错误，而“症状”在医学语言中是指病人自己的感受。外部观察，无论多么敏锐，都是迹象。头痛是一种症状。医生用听诊器听到的胸部声音是一种迹象。咳嗽既是症状，也是迹象。
strangely dissonant imagery tells also of a troubled soul who found reality harsh—so harsh that the mind had to be fragmented in order to fragment the pain.
奇怪的不和谐图像也讲述了一个烦恼的灵魂，他发现现实是残酷的——非常残酷，以至于心灵不得不被分裂，以分散痛苦。
absence of mind is one cause of the distractibility and short attention spans that bedevil the adult or child with ADD, except around activities of high interest and motivation. ere is an almost active notnoticing, as if a person purposefully went out of her way to be oblivious of what is around her.
心不在焉是导致分心和注意力短暂的原因之一，这困扰着患有ADD的成年人或儿童，除了在具有高度兴趣和动力的活动中。这里几乎是一种积极的不注意，就好像一个人故意忽视周围的事物。
e distractibility fosters chaos. You decide to clean your room, which,typically, looks like a tornado has just passed through. You pick a book oﬀ the ﬂoor and move to replace it on the shelf. As you do so, you notice that two volumes of poetry by William Carlos Williams are not stacked side by side. Forgetting the debris on the ﬂoor, you lift one of the volumes to place it beside its companion. Turning a page, you begin to read a poem. e poem has a classical reference in it, which prompts you to consult your guide to Greek mythology; now you are lost because one reference leads to another. An hour later, your interest in classical mythology exhausted for the moment, you return to your intended task. You are hunting for the missing half of a pair of socks that has gone on furlough, perhapspermanently, when another item of clothing on the ﬂoor reminds you that you have laundry to wash before the evening. As you head downstairs,laundry hamper in arm, the telephone rings. Your plan to create order in your room is now doomed. 注意力涣散会引发混乱。你决定打扫自己的房间，你的房间通常就像刚刚龙卷风刮过一样。你从地板上捡起一本书，把它放回书架上。就在这时，你注意到威廉·卡洛斯·威廉姆斯的两本诗集没有并排摆放。你把注意力从地板上的杂物上移开，拿起其中一本诗集把它放在另一本旁边。翻开一页，你开始读一首诗。这首诗引用了古典作品，这促使你去查阅你的希腊神话指南；现在你迷路了，因为一条注释引出了另一条注释。一个小时后，你对古典神话的兴趣暂时耗尽，你回到了你想要完成的任务上。你在寻找一双失踪的
袜子，这双袜子可能永远失踪了，这时，地板上的另一件衣服提醒你，在晚上之前，你还有衣服要洗。当你拿着装满脏衣服的洗衣篮下楼时，电话响了。你在房间里创造秩序的计划现在注定要失败了。
Completely lacking in the ADD mind is a template for order, a mental 多动症患者的头脑中完全缺乏一种有序的模板，一种精神秩序。model of how order comes about.
Active attention is a capacity the ADD brain lacks whenever organized work must be done, or when attention needs to be directed toward something of low interest.
主动注意力是多动症患者的大脑在需要完成有组织的工作，或需要将注意力集中在缺乏兴趣的事物上时所欠缺的能力。
A facility for focusing when one is interested in something does not rule out ADD, but to be able to focus, the person with ADD needs a much higher level of motivation than do other people.
当一个人对某事感兴趣时，能够集中注意力，这并不能排除注意力缺陷多动症，但要能够集中注意力，多动症患者需要比其他人高得多的动机水平。
e second nearly ubiquitous characteristic of ADD is impulsiveness of 注意缺陷多动障碍的第二个几乎无处不在的特点是冲动性。word or deed, with poorly controlled emotional reactivity.
无论言行，情绪反应都难以控制
Hyperactivity is the third salient characteristic of ADD.
”超兴奋是ADD的第三个显著特征。
e individual with ADD experiences the mind as a perpetual-motion 患有ADD的人将思想视为永动机machine.
An intense aversion to boredom, an abhorrence of it, takes hold as soon as there is no ready focus of activity, distraction or attention. An unremitting lack of stillness is felt internally—a constant background static in the brain, a ceaseless “white noise,” as Dr. John Ratey, a Harvardpsychiatrist, has put it. Merciless pressure in the mind impells withoutspeciﬁc aim or direction. As long ago as 1934, an article in e New England Journal of Medicine identiﬁed a distressing quality to some people’slives, which the authors called “organic drivenness.” I, for one, have rarely had a moment’s relaxation without the immediate and troubling feeling that I ought to be doing something else instead. Like father, like son. At the age of eight or nine, my son said to me, “I always think I should be doing something, but I don’t know what it is.” e oldest person to whom I have prescribed a stimulant was an eighty-ﬁve-year-old woman who, on taking Ritalin, was able to sit still more than ﬁfteen minutes for the veryﬁrst time in her life. 机器。一旦没有现成的活动、分心之事或注意对象，对无聊的强烈厌恶感就会占据上风，让人无法忍受。内心感觉不到丝毫的平静——就像哈佛精神病学家约翰·瑞迪博士所说的，大脑中持续不断的“白噪音”，一种无休止的“白噪音”。无情的精神压力驱使着人们，却没有特定的目标或方向。早在1934年，《新英格兰医学期刊》上的一篇文章就指出了某些人的生活中存在一种令人痛苦的特质，作者称之为“内在驱动力”。就我而言，如果没有马上去做其他事情的紧迫感，我几乎无法放松片刻。父亲如此，儿子亦然。八九岁的时候，我儿子对我说：“我总觉得我应该做点什么，但我不知道该做什么。”给我开过兴奋
剂的最年长的人是一位85岁的女士，她服用利他林后，生平第一次能够静坐15分钟以上。
e restlessness coexists with long periods of procrastination. e threat of failure or the promise of reward has to be immediate for the motivation apparatus to be turned on. Without the rousing adrenaline rush of racing against time, inertia prevails. Not once in high school or university did I begin an assignment or essay before the eve of the day it was due.
躁动不安与长时间的拖延共存。只有面临失败的威胁或看到获得回报的希望，激励机制才会被启动。如果没有与时间的赛跑所带来的令人兴奋的肾上腺素飙升，惰性就会占据上风。在高中或大学期间，我从来没有在截止日期的前一天开始写作业或论文。
On the other hand,when there is something one wants, neither patience nor procrastination exist. One has to do it, get it, have it, experience it, immediately.
另一方面，当一个人想要某样东西时，耐心和拖延都不复存在。一个人必须去做，去得到它，拥有它，体验它，立刻马上。
“I am a person of permanentpotential,” one patient said. Surges of initial enthusiasm quickly ebb. People report unﬁnished retainer walls begun over a decade ago, partly constructed boats taking up garage space year after year, courses begun andquit, books half read, business ventures forsaken, stories or poetryunwritten—many, many roads not traveled. “我是一个有永久潜力的人”，一位患者说。最初的热情很快就消退了。人们报告说，十多年前开始建造的未完工的挡土墙，年复一年地占用车库空间的部分建造的船只，开始学习然后放弃的课程，读了一半的书，放弃的商业冒险，未写的故事或诗歌——许多许多没有走过的路。
An adult with ADD looks back on his life to see countless plans never 一个患有ADD的成年人回顾他的生活，看到无数从未实现的计划。fully realized and intentions unfulﬁlled.
Social skills are also an issue. Something about ADD hinders the capacity 社交技能也是一个问题。多动症会妨碍能力to recognize interpersonal boundaries.
is sense of being always on the outside looking in,of somehow missing the point, is pervasive. At social events, I tend to gravitate to the periphery, conscious of a feeling that somehow I cannot enter into the spirit of things. I observe people talking to each other, people I may know quite well, acutely aware that I have nothing to say to anyone. Social conversation has always been a mystery to me. I have at times looked at people engaged in animated discussion and wished that I was invisible so that I could overhear them—not to eavesdrop but to ﬁnd out once and for all exactly what there is to talk about. My patients with ADD tell me much the same thing about their experience. “I don’t know how to make smalltalk, or I’m afraid of saying something stupid,” a twenty-six-year-old woman said. And the truth is, when the ADD adult does join conversations,she often ﬁnds herself bored by the minute attention others devote to subjects that to her seem to skim only the surface.
被排斥在外的感觉无处不在。在社交活动中，我倾向于置身事外，意识到自己不知为何无法融入其中。我观察人们相互交谈，这些人我可能非常了解，我敏锐地意识到我对任何人都没有什么可说的。社交交谈对我来说一直是个谜。我有时看着人们热烈讨论，真希望自己是隐形的，这样我就可以偷听他们说话——不是为了偷听，而是为了一劳永逸地弄清楚到底有什么可说的。我的多动症患者也告诉我很多关于他们经历的同样的事情。“我不知道如何闲聊，或者我害怕说一些愚蠢的话，”一个26岁的女人说。事实上，当多动症成人加入谈话时，她经常发现自己对别人投入的话题感到厌烦，对她来说，这些话题似乎只是浮光掠影。
e common theme on all days, good or bad, is a gnawing sense of having missed out on something important in life. 在所有的日子里，无论是好是坏，都有一个共同的主题，那就是对生活中错过重要事情的痛苦感觉。
ADD is not a problem of knowing what to do; it is a problem of doing what you know.—RUSSELL A. BARKLEY, PH.D., “Improved Delayed Responding”
IT IS NOT THAT I WISH to be late. I do not imagine for a moment that I 注意缺陷障碍不是不知道该做什么的问题，而是知道该做什么却不去做的问题。——拉塞尔·A·巴克利博士，《改善延迟反应》我并不是想迟到。我一刻也没有想过我会迟到。will be late.
I may have to be somewhere, miles away, at 9:00 a.m., but as long as it is not yet nine, I fully believe I have time enough. I am scheduled to attend ward rounds with nurses and other physicians at Vancouver Hospital. At 8:50 I leap into the shower, still conﬁdent: there is space between the big hand of the watch and the hour marker, so I am not late. 要迟到了。我可能得在早上9点出现在几英里之外的地方，但只要还没到9点，我就完全相信自己还有足够的时间。我计划和温哥华医院的护士和其他医生一起查房。8点50分，我跳进淋浴间，仍然自信满满：手表的大针和时标之间还有空隙，所以我不会迟到。at the traveling always takes longer than I expect, that ice will have to be scraped oﬀ the car, that I will not ﬁnd the keys, that I may get stuck in traﬃc do not arise in the mind as concrete possibilities. Two thought systems vie for control in the brain: the one logical and aware, the other the immature time sense of a young child. e latter is most often dominant. 在旅行中，我总是比预期花费更长的时间，冰必须从车上刮下来，我找不到钥匙，我可能会被困在交通中，这些都不在我的脑海中成为具体的可能性。两个思想系统在大脑中争夺控制权：一个是逻辑和意识，另一个是年轻孩子不成熟的时间感。后者通常占主导地位。
It’s only when nine o’clock strikes and I am searching for my car keys that irritability begins to set in. When I get outside and realize that frost has made the windshield completely opaque, I begin to curse. By the time I have to rush back up the stairs once, then twice, to ﬁnd briefcase or lunch or stethoscope, I feel utterly frustrated. 直到9点的钟声敲响，我开始寻找汽车钥匙时，我才感到烦躁不安。当我走到外面，发现霜冻使挡风玻璃完全不透明时，我开始咒骂起来。当我不得不一次又一次地冲上楼梯，寻找公文包、午餐或听诊器时，我感到彻底的沮丧。I arrive on the ward ﬁfteen minutes late—on a good day—removing my coat and hat and pulling my rubber galoshes oﬀ each foot in turn while hopping hurriedly down the hall on the other. Taking a deep breath outside the door, I steady myself. I enter the meeting room. Rounds are already in progress. “Okay, we can begin,” I say. I notice that not everyone laughs. 我到达病房时已经迟到了 15 分钟——在顺利的情况下——我脱下外套和帽子，把橡胶套鞋从每只脚上一个一个地脱下来，同时匆忙地跳着穿过走廊。在门外深吸一口气，我稳定了一下自己。我走进会议室。查房已经开始。“好的，我们可以开始了，”我说。我注意到不是每个人都在笑。Every adult with ADD can relate such anecdotes—funny to tell, not so 每个患有ADD的成年人都可以讲这样的轶事——有趣的是，不是那么有趣。
funny to experience and never so jolly for others inconvenienced by the tardiness and disorganization. e ADD mind is aﬄicted by a sort of time illiteracy, or what Dr. Russell Barkley has called “time blindness.” One is either hopelessly short of time, dashing about like a deaf bat, or else acts as if blessed with the gift of eternity. It’s as if one’s time sense never developed past a stage other people leave behind in early childhood. 体验起来很有趣，但对那些因迟钝和无序而感到不便的人来说，这从来都不是一件愉快的事。ADD患者的思维受到一种“时间文盲”的困扰，或者用罗素·巴克利（Russell Barkley）博士的话来说，这是一种“时间失明”。一个人要么绝望地感到时间不够用，像失聪的蝙蝠一样到处乱撞，要么表现得好像被赐予了永恒的天赋。就好像一个人的时间感从未发展到别人在童年早期就遗留下来的阶段。To the very young child, any block of time seems inﬁnite. Tell her that supper will be ready in three minutes: desperate wailing will signal her conviction that you have sentenced her to starvation. Tell her to hurry because time is running out, and she will not know what you are talking about. How can inﬁnity run out? Only two units of time exist for the smallchild: the now and the not-now. e not-now is inﬁnity. 对于年幼的孩子来说，任何一段固定的时间似乎都是无穷无尽的。告诉她说，晚餐将在 3 分钟后做好：绝望的哭喊声会让她确信，你把她判处了饥饿的刑罚。告诉她要快点，因为时间不多了，而她根本不知道你在说什么。时间
怎么可能用完呢？对于年幼的孩子来说，只有两个时间单位：现在和不是现在。而“不是现在”就是无穷无尽的。e time sense of the ADD adult or child is warped in other ways. Ask 患有ADD的成人或孩子的时间感在其他方面也发生了扭曲。people with ADD how long it will take to perform a particular task, and they will notoriously underestimate. A kind of magical thinking dominates,characteristic of young children: if I will it, it will happen. In magic anything is possible. Castles can be built or destroyed with the wave of awand, worlds traversed in seven-league boots, you can get from Oz to Kansas by clicking your heels together. Magic vanquishes time. 患有注意力缺陷多动症的人需要多长时间才能完成某项任务，他们通常会低估。有一种神奇的思维占主导地位，这是儿童的特点：如果我想，它就会发生。在魔术中，一切皆有可能。城堡可以随着魔杖的挥动而建造或毁灭，你可以穿着七里靴穿越世界，你可以通过跺脚从奥兹国到达堪萨斯州。魔法战胜了时间。No infant is born with a sense of time. e gradual acquisition of time sense is a task of development that begins in early childhood. At the outset,the infant has no categories such as time, space or causality, no awareness that one event leads to another. It is not until the age of seven or so, Jean Piaget found, that children begin to have a full understanding of time as a continuous ﬂow. Until then, the child is in what Piaget, the great Swiss cognitive psychologist, called the “preop
erational stage,” when everything is observed and interpreted from only one point of view, the child’s. “epreoperational child, in his egocentric manner, believes that he can stoptime, speed it up, or slow it down.”1 e networks of nerve cells responsible for the various brain activities do not all develop the same way,at the same time, or, necessarily, to the same degree. With ADD we witness a delayed or permanently arrested maturation of the balanced time sense most people achieve by adulthood. In attention deﬁcit disorder, the circuitry of time intelligence is underdeveloped. 没有一个婴儿生来就有时间感，对时间感的逐渐获得是儿童从早期开始的一项发展任务。最初，婴儿没有时间、空间或因果关系等概念，没有一件事导致另一件事的意识。让·皮亚杰发现，直到7岁左右，儿童才开始完全理解时间是一种持续的流动。在此之前，儿童处于瑞士伟大的认知心理学家皮亚杰所称的“前运算阶段”，在这个阶段，一切都是从一个角度观察和解释的，即儿童自己的角度。“前运算阶段的儿童，以自我为中心，认为他可以停止时间，加快时间或减慢时间。”1负责各种大脑活动的神经元网络的发展方式并不相同，也不是同时发生，或者不一定以同样的程度发生。在注意力缺陷多动障碍中，时间智能回路发育不良。
Underdevelopment best explains another time-related malfunction of the ADD brain, the chronic incapacity to consider the future. e guiding assumption of the adult with attention deﬁcit disorder, like that of the small child, seems to be that only the present exists and needs to be taken into account. He lives as if his actions had no implications for the future,no eﬀects on future needs, relationships or responsibilities. e short-term goal is invariably chosen over the long-term, with the exception of activities or projects capable of arousing the sluggish motivation-reward nexus in the brain. e present impulse dominates. It has been aptly said that people with ADD forget to remember the future. In the moment of action or decision making, ADD adults are no more mindful of consequences than a young child. 发育不良最好地解释了ADD大脑的另一个与时间相关的故障，即慢性无法考虑未来。对于患有注意力缺陷障碍的成年人来说，与年幼的孩子一样，似乎只有现在存在，并且需要加以考虑。他生活得好像他的行为对未来没有任何影响，对未来的需求、关系或责任没有任何影响。短期目标总是优先于长期目标，除非活动或项目能够激发大脑中迟钝的动机-奖励联系。现在冲动占主导地位。有人恰如其分地说，患有ADD的人忘记记住未来。在行动或决策时，ADD成年人对后果的意识并不比年幼的孩子强。
In general, we may speak of an underdevelopment of emotional intelligence. In his best-selling book Emotional Intelligence, DanielGoleman, behavioral and brain sciences writer for e New York Times,deﬁnes this capacity as “being able to motivate oneself and persist in the face of frustrations; to control impulse and to delay gratiﬁcation; to regulate one’s moods and keep distress from swamping the ability to think …”2 We have only to place a negative qualiﬁer before the “being able” in that sentence, as in “not being able,” and we arrive at a succinct description of the ADD personality. 总的来说，我们可能会说情感智力的发展不足。丹尼尔·戈尔曼是《纽约时报》的行为和大脑科学作家，在他的畅销书《情感智力》中，他将这种能力定义为“能够在面对挫折时激励自己并坚持下去；能够控制冲动并延迟满足感；能够调节自己的情绪，不让沮丧淹没思考的能力……”2我们只需要在这句话中“能够”的前面加上一个否定词，比如“不能”，就能简洁地描述出注意力缺陷多动症患者的性格。
ADD is also found more commonly in people whose ﬁrst-degree relatives are alcoholics or suﬀer from depression, anxiety, addiction, obsessive-compulsive disorder or Tourette’s syndrome. It may appear from such facts that this motley collection of related syndromes is largely hereditary—but to assume that would be like believing that if there are three generations of butchers or bakers or candlestick makers in a family, then meat cutting,baking and candle manufacturing must also be genetic. e family atmosphere in which the child spends the early formative years has a major impact on brain development. It is obvious that brain/mind problems such as ADD are far more likely to develop in families where the parents are struggling with dysfunction or psychological problems of their own. It would be astonishing if children growing up in such unsettled environments did not develop some of the same problems. No genes need be involved at all for these conditions to run in families.2 在一级亲属中有酗酒者或患抑郁症、焦虑症、上瘾症、强迫症或抽动秽语综合征的人中，ADHD也较为常见。从这些事实中，我们可能会认为这些相关综合症大多具有遗传性——但这种假设就像认为如果一个家庭中有三代屠夫、三代糕点师或三代烛台匠，那么肉类切割、糕点制作和蜡烛制造也一定是遗传的一样。孩子在早期成长过程中所处的家庭氛围对其大脑发育有着重要影响。很明显，ADD等大脑/心理问题更有可能在父母本身就有功能障碍或心理问题的家庭中发展。如果成长在这样的不稳定环境中的孩子没有出现一些同样的问题，那将令人惊讶。这些情况在家庭中遗
传时，不需要涉及任何基因。
Sensitivity is the reason why allergies are more common among ADD 敏感性是过敏在多动症患者中更常见的原因
ey both are expressions of the same underlying inborntrait: sensitivity. Since emotionally hypersensitive reactions are no less physiological than the body’s allergic responses to physical substances, we may say truthfully that people with ADD have emotional allergies.
两者都是同一潜在内在特质的表现：敏感。由于情绪敏感反应与身体对物理物质的过敏反应一样生理，我们可以实事求是地说，多动症患者有情绪过敏。
Almost any parent with an ADD child, or any adult living with an ADD 几乎任何有ADD孩子的父母，或者任何有ADD的成年人spouse, will have noticed in the ADD person a touchiness, a “thin skin.” People with ADD are forever told that they are “too sensitive” or that they should stop being “so touchy.” One might as well advise a child with hay fever to stop being “so allergic.” With its usual wisdom, everyday language has found an accurate description of hypersensitivity when it speaks of someone having a thin skin. If one had an area on one’s thigh with part of the epidermis destroyedby, say, scalding hot water, one literally would have a thin skin: the nerve endings would be closer to the surface. A slight gust of air might cause a highly unpleasant sensation, even pain, whereas surfaces with full-thickness skin would feel little or nothing. e emotionally sensitive person lives, as it were, with the nerve endings that send emotional stimuli to the brain centers very close to the surface. Like the exposed nerve endings in scaldedskin, they are very easily irritated. Hence, my daughter’s complaint that I was yelling. Of course, I was the short-tempered father in the anecdote.e suppertime set-to used to be familiar in our home. 配偶会注意到ADD患者有点敏感，有点“薄皮肤”。患有ADD的人总是被告知他们“太敏感”或者他们应该停止“这么敏感”。这就像建议一个患有花粉热的孩子停止“这么过敏”一样。日常语言以其特有的智慧，在谈到某人“薄
皮肤”时，准确描述了超敏反应。如果一个人的大腿上有部分表皮被滚烫的热水破坏，那么他真的会有“薄皮肤”：神经末梢会靠近表面。轻微的气流可能会引起非常不快的感觉，甚至是疼痛，而表皮完整的皮肤几乎感觉不到。情感敏感的人就像生活在神经末梢中，这些神经末梢会将情感刺激发送到距离表面很近的大脑中心。就像烫伤皮肤中裸露的神经末梢一样，它们很容易被激怒。因此，我女儿抱怨我大喊大叫。当然，我是这个轶事中的脾气暴躁的父亲。
Like hypersensitive instruments, sensitive children register and record even minute changes in their emotional environment. It is not a matter of choice for them; their nervous systems react. It is as if they had invisible antennae projecting in every direction, picking up and conducting into their bodies and their minds the psychic emanations around them. ey may have no conscious knowledge of this, any more than an instrument is consciously aware of what measurements it is registering. Unlikeinstruments, however, the sensory equipment of human beings is not easily shut oﬀ.
就像超敏感的仪器一样，敏感的孩子甚至能察觉和记录他们所处情感环境中的细微变化。对他们来说，这不是一个选择的问题；他们的神经系统会做出反应。这就像他们有无形的触角，可以感知周围的心理辐射，并将其导入自己的身体和头脑中。他们可能没有意识到这一点，就像仪器没有意识到自己在记录什么测量结果一样。然而，与仪器不同的是，人类的感官设备不容易关闭。
e existence of sensitive people is an advantage for humankind because it is this group that best expresses humanity’s creative urges and needs. rough their instinctual responses the world is best interpreted. Under normal circumstances, they are artists or artisans,seekers, inventors, shamans, poets, prophets. ere would be valid and powerful evolutionary reasons for the survival of genetic material coding for sensitivity. It is not diseases that are being inherited but a trait of intrinsic survival value to human beings. Sensitivity is transmuted intosuﬀering and disorders only when the world is unable to heed the exquisitely tuned physiological and psychic responses of the sensitive individual.
敏感人群的存在对人类来说是一种优势，因为正是这一人群最能表达人类创造性的冲动和需求。通过他们的本能反应，我们可以对世界做出最好的诠释。在正常情况下，他们是艺术家、工匠、探索者、发明家、萨满、诗人、先知。因此，敏感的遗传物质之所以能够生存下来，有着合理且强大的进化原因。被遗传的并不是疾病，而是对人类具有内在生存价值的特质。只有当世界无法注意到敏感个体微调的生理和心理反应时，敏感性才会转化为干扰和紊乱。
e tense or depressed mothering adult will not be able to accompany the infant intorelaxed, happy spaces. He may also not fully pick up signs of the infant’s emotional distress, or may not be able to respond to them as eﬀectively as he would wish. e ADD child’s diﬃculty reading social cues likely originates from her relationship cues not being read by the nurturing adult,who was distracted by stress.
紧张或抑郁的母亲将无法陪伴婴儿进入放松、快乐的空间。他可能也无法完全捕捉到婴儿情绪痛苦的迹象，或者无法像自己希望的那样有效地回应这些迹象。患有注意力缺陷多动症儿童在解读社交线索方面的困难可能源于抚养成人被压力分心，无法解读她的关系线索。
Infants whose caregivers were too stressed, for whatever reason, to give them the necessary attunement contact will grow up with a chronic tendency to feel alone with their emotions, to have a sense—rightly orwrongly—that no one can share how they feel, that no one can“understand.” Attunement is the quintessential component of a larger process, called attachment.7 Attachment is simply our need to be close to somebody. I
照顾者因为各种原因而压力过大，无法给予婴儿必要的情感联系，这些婴儿长大后会长期感到自己的情感是孤独的，并产生一种感觉——无论正确与否——即没有人能分享他们的感受，没有人能“理解”他们。情感联系是更广泛的过程——依恋——的精髓所在。7 依恋只是我们亲近某人的需要。
ADD children whose needs for warm parental contact are most frustrated grow up to be adults with the most severe cases of ADD. 最需要温暖父母接触的ADD儿童长大后患有最严重的ADD。
Nature’s goal for human growth is for the eventual maturation of a self-motivated, self-regulated and self-reliant adult. e infant lacks these attributes. We may say that the natural agenda is really the transformation of regulation from dependence on another individual to independence,from external regulation to internal regulation.
大自然对人类成长的目标是培养出一个具有自我激励能力、能够自我调节且独立自主的成年人。婴儿还缺乏这些特质。可以说，自然的成长过程实际上就是将调节方式从依赖他人转变为独立自主，从外部调节转变为内部调节。
When visual-spatial orientation is impaired, a person tends to bump his head a lot or run into people unseeingly and have diﬃculty following physicaldirections—all features of ADD I am intimately familiar with. 视觉空间定向能力在帮助人们确定物体在空间中的位置方面起着关键作用。当这种能力受损时，一个人往往会频繁撞到头部，或者不知不觉地与他人相撞，并且难以理解或遵循具体的方向指示——这些都是我非常熟悉的注意力缺陷多动障碍（ADD）的症状。
When I ﬁrst took an antidepressant in mymidforties, I was amazed at the diﬀerence. Curiously, I felt much more like myself. It was as if a fog had evaporated and I saw that for years previously I’d had only periodic glimpses of a life not burdened by negative feelings.
当我四十多岁时第一次开始服用抗抑郁药时，我惊讶地发现自己的状态有了显著变化。有趣的是，我感觉自己仿佛找回了真正的自我。就好像一层迷雾突然消散了，我这才意识到：在过去的那些年里，我其实只是偶尔才能瞥见一种没有负面情绪困扰的生活。
Family strife and parental depression contribute to the child’s ADD 家庭矛盾和父母的抑郁情绪会加剧孩子的注意力缺陷多动障碍（ADD）。
In the language of psychology, mental absence, tuning out, is an example of a mind state known as dissociation.1 It is employed in clinical psychiatry to refer to speciﬁc syndromes such as multiple personalitydisorder, but I use the term in its general sense. Dissociation, including the tuning-out of ADD, originates in a defensive need—it is a form of psychological defense. Gloucester’s motive to be “distract,” in the fourth act of King Lear, is very close to the source of the “distractness” of ADD. It is a way of coping with emotional hurt. e original purpose of dissociation is to separate conscious awareness from some emotional pain we areexperiencing, to dis-associate one from the other. We may think of dissociation as a psychological anesthetic. 从心理学的角度讲，心不在焉、神思恍惚是一种分离性失神发作。1在临床精神病学中，它被用来指代多重人格障碍等特定综合征，但我在此使用这个术语的一般意义。分离性失神发作，包括注意力缺陷多动障碍的心不在焉症状，起源于一种防御性需求，是一种心理防御机制。在《李尔王》第四幕中，格洛斯特想要“心烦意乱”的动机，与注意力缺陷多动障碍的“心烦意乱”症状的根源非常接近。这是一种应对情感伤害的方式。分离性失神发作的原始目的是将我们的意识知觉与我们正在经历的一些情感痛苦分离，使两者互不关联。我们可以将分离性失神发作视为一种心理麻醉剂。
Episodic experiences of a distressing nature do not inducedissociation, but chronic distress does—the distress of the sensitive infant with unsatisﬁed attunement needs, for example. e infant has to dissociate chronic emotional pain from consciousness for two reasons.First, it is too overwhelming for his fragile nervous system. He simply cannot exist in what we might call a state of chronic negative arousal, with adrenaline and other stress hormones pumping through his veins all the time. It is physiologically too toxic. He has to block it out. Second, if the parent’s anxiety is the source of the infant’s distress, the infant unconsciously senses that fully expressing his own emotional turmoil will only heighten that anxiety. His distress would then be aggravated—a vicious cycle he can escape by tuning out.
令人痛苦的经历不会引起“分离”，但长期的痛苦会——例如，敏感的婴儿在得不到情感上的满足时所感到的痛苦。婴儿必须将慢性情感痛苦从意识中分离出来有两个原因。首先，对于他脆弱的神经系统来说，这种痛苦太强烈了。他不能一直处于我们所说的慢性负面唤起状态，肾上腺素和其他应激激素会一直冲击他的静脉。生理上来说，这太有毒了。他必须将其排除在外。第二，如果父母的焦虑是婴儿痛苦的原因，婴儿会无意识地感觉到，充分表达自己的情绪波动只会加剧这种焦虑。他的痛苦就会加重——他可以通过不听来逃脱这种恶性循环。
e survival value of all psychological defenses is relatively short-lived. 所有心理防御的生存价值都是相对短暂的。
Sooner or later dissociation becomes a hindrance, interferes with life and slows psychological growth. e tuned-out individual has diﬃculty learning from experience and is hampered in making contact with others.e inner retreat from psychological reality means that he may never learn to cope with emotional setbacks in a creative and positive way. We may compare any psychological defense to a heavy fur coat a man in a cold northern climate puts on to keep from freezing. Wearing the fur coat saves his life. Let’s imagine that he is suddenly transported to the equator, coat still on. He would soon ﬁnd that the clothing that helped him survive in the north makes him most uncomfortable or even ill in his new circumstances. Of course, our traveler is free to remove his coat. e problem with unconscious psychological defenses is that they cannot be shed at will. ey were induced without our conscious will in the ﬁrstplace, indeed before we had any will at all. 分离迟早会成为一种障碍，干扰生活，减缓心理成长。自我封闭的人难以从经验中学习，在与他人交往方面受到阻碍。从心理上远离现实意味着，他可能永远无法学会以一种创造性和积极的方式应对情感挫折。我们可以把任何一种心理防御机制比作一个人在寒冷的北方穿上厚厚的皮大衣以避免冻僵。穿上皮大衣救了他的命。让我们想象一下，他突然被转移到赤道，皮大衣还穿在身上。他很快就会发现，在北方帮助他生存下来的衣服，在新环境中让他感到最不舒服，甚至生病。当然，我们的旅行者可以自由地脱下他的外套。无意识心理防御的问题在
于，它们不能随意摆脱。它们首先是在我们没有意识的情况下诱导的，事实上，在我们有任何意愿之前就诱导了。
Once in place, a defense mechanism such as tuning out takes on a life of its own. Little or nothing of a distressing nature need be there in the immediate environment for it to happen. It becomes, as it were, the“default” setting in the cerebral apparatus of awareness: unless some other special switch is turned on, tuned out will be the state that the brain automatically returns to. Because tuning out is based on deeply entrenched neurological responses, their later activation requires very little stimulus. Once a circuit is established, signals will travel along it much more easily than along alternative routes, in a manner analogous to the ease of walking along a beaten path rather than through grass or bush on either side of it,or water ﬂowing in a channel instead of across ﬂat ground. If we want the stream to run in a diﬀerent direction, we will have to create new courses for it. 一旦建立，像调出这样的防御机制就会自行发挥作用。即使周围环境没有令人痛苦的事情发生，它也会发生。可以说，它成为了大脑意识机制的“默认”设置：除非打开其他特殊开关，否则大脑会自动恢复到“调出”状态。由于“调出”是基于根深蒂固的神经反应，因此它们后来的激活只需要很少的刺激。一旦电路建立，信号沿着它比沿着替代路线更容易传播，就像沿着一条小路走比穿过它两侧的草丛或灌木丛，或者在渠道中流动比在平地上流动更容易一样。如果我们想让溪
流流向不同的方向，我们将不得不为它创造新的路线。
Although we think of attention as a function of the intellect, its deepest roots are in the subsoil of emotion.
虽然我们认为注意力是智力的功能，但它的根源在情感的深层。
Emotions remain the basis ofattention, even after the intellect comes to dominate our conscious thought.
即使在智力主导我们的意识思维之后，情感仍然是注意力的基础。
In fact, there is a close link between the visual and attentionalsystems: a powerful incentive for the development of both is the infant’s emotional relationship with the mothering adult.
事实上，视觉和注意力系统之间有着紧密的联系：婴儿与照顾他的大人的情感关系是促进这两个系统发展的强大动力。
eir brain’sattentional system cannot switch into “schoolwork mode” when it is consumed by anxieties about the child’s emotional connection with the world.
他们的注意力系统被孩子与世界的情感联系所困扰时，他们的注意力系统无法切换到“学校作业模式”。
For people deeply hurt, the internal world may oﬀer more meaning than the real one. A woman in her thirties whose ADD was never noticed because she was not hyperactive, only a day-dreamer, told me that she spent entire school days staring out the window, lost in fanciful adventures with imaginary friends. From the outside, one might have described her as“distracted.” e Latin root of distract is “draw away”—drawing her away from the inside was her hiddenmost emotional longing. Her brain unconsciously assigned greater value to a self-created internal universe than to anything or anyone in the classroom. 对于那些深受伤害的人来说，内心世界可能比现实世界更有意义。一位三十多岁的女性从未被注意到患有ADD，因为她不是多动症，而只是一个白日梦者，她告诉我，她在整个求学过程中都盯着窗外，沉浸在与想象中的朋友的异想天开的冒险中。从外界来看，她可能会被描述为“注意力不集中”。分散的拉丁词根是“拉走”——将她从内心深处拉走是她隐藏最深的情感渴望。她的大脑无意识地将更大的价值赋予了一个自我创造的内心世界，而不是课堂上的任何事物或任何人。
e nagging hunger for emotional contact explains the oft-observed“paradox” that many children with ADD are capable of focused work in the presence of an adult who is keeping them company and paying attention to them. is is no paradox at all, if we see the opposing roles of anxiety and attachment in inﬂuencing attention: attachment promotes attention,anxiety undermines it. When the child is not concerned with seeking emotional contact, his prefrontal cortex is freed to allocate attention to the task at hand, illustrating that what we call attention deﬁcit disorder is not aﬁxed, unalterable physiological state; it’s a physiological state, yes, but notﬁxed and unalterable. e warmth and satisfaction of positive contact with the adult is often just as good as a psychostimulant in supplying the child’s prefrontal cortex with dopamine. Greater security means less anxiety and more focused attention. e unseen factor that remains constant in all situations is the child’s unconscious yearning for attachment, dating back to the ﬁrst years of life. Where this need is satisﬁed, ADD problems begin to recede. 对情感联系的强烈渴望解释了经常观察到的“悖论”，即许多患有注意力缺陷多动症（ADD）的儿童在有成年人陪伴并关注他们的情况下能够集中精力工作。如果我们看到焦虑和依恋在影响注意力方面的对立角色，那么这根本不是悖论：依恋促进注意力，焦虑破坏注意力。当孩子不关心寻求情感联系时，他的前额叶皮层就获得了自由，可以将注意力分配到手头的任务上，这表明我们所说的注意力缺陷障碍不是一种固定的、不可改变的生理状态；它是一种生理状态，是的，但不是固定的、不可改变的。与成人进行积极
联系时的温暖和满足感，往往就像在为孩子的前额叶皮层提供多巴胺一样有效。更大的安全感意味着更少的焦虑和更专注的注意力。一个在所有情况下都保持不变的不可见因素是孩子对依恋的无意识渴望，这种渴望可以追溯到生命的最初几年。当这种需求得到满足时，多动症问题开始消退。
when our attentional resources are consumed by internal thoughts and feelings, there are few left over for dealing with the world outside.”7
当我们的注意力被内心的想法和感受消耗殆尽时，就没有多少剩余的注意力来应对外部世界了。”7
I do not know if you have noticed that the moment you cease to be active, there is immediately a feeling of nervous apprehension; you feel as though you are not alive, notalert, so you must keep going. And there is fear of being alone, of going for a walk alone,of being by yourself, without a book, without a radio, without talking; the fear of sitting quietly without doing something all the time with your hands or with your mind or with your heart. 我不知道你是否注意过，当你不再活跃时，立刻会有一种紧张不安的感觉；你会觉得自己没有活着，没有警觉，所以你必须继续前进。还有害怕孤独，害怕独自散步，害怕独自一人，没有书，没有收音机，没有交谈；害怕静静地坐着，不做任何事情，无论是用你的手，用你的思想，还是用你的心。
Hyperactivity is experienced in a number of ways. e person with ADD 多动症可以通过多种方式体验到。一个有注意力缺陷多动症的人feels discomfort at having to keep still for even short periods of time. ere may be a physical inability to refrain from restless movements for more than a few minutes. Always, one is caught in a mental whirlwind. A seventy-two-year-old man, a geologist, called it “newsreel thinking,” by which he meant the rapid shifting of his thoughts from one subject to another. (He had originally trained as an engineer but could not abide theconﬁnes of an oﬃce. He switched to geology because the work allowed him to be outdoors, on the move.) Another sign of hyperactivity may be frequent movement of the eyes, a scanning of the environment that frustrates other people. It is disconcerting to be with an individual who seems to be always on the lookout for something, or someone, else. 即使是短时间保持静止也会感到不适。这可能是因为身体无法在几分钟内抑制住不安分的动作。总是，一个人会陷入精神风暴。一位 72 岁的地质学家称其为“新闻片式思维”，他的意思是他的思维会从一件事迅速转移到另一件事上。（他最初接受的是工程师培训，但无法忍受办公室的束缚。他转向地质学是因为这项工作允许他在户外活动。）过度活跃的另一个表现可能是频繁地移动眼睛，对
环境进行扫描，这会令其他人感到沮丧。和一个似乎总是在寻找其他东西或其他人的家伙在一起，会令人感到不安。
ere is another component to hyperactivity: throughout life, it continues to be a human response during times of high anxiety. If you were told that in the next week, at some unpredictable moment, some unnamed disaster would strike you or one close to you, and that you were helpless to do anything to prevent it, your response would quite probably resemble the habitual mental and physical behaviors of the hyperactive child or adult with ADD. YOU would have diﬃculty focusing your thoughts, and your mind might feel like a squirrel on a treadmill: racing but not going anywhere. Sitting still would be a chore. A clichéd image of helpless anxiety in an adult is the father pacing nervously outside the delivery room where his wife is giving birth. I believe hyperactivity in ADD is fed by a current of permanent, subterranean anxiety. 这里是多动症另一个组成部分：在人的一生中，当处于高度焦虑状态时，多动症会持续存在。如果你被告知，在未来的一周内，在某个不可预知的时刻，某种不知名的灾难将会降临到你或你身边亲近的人身上，而且你对此无能为力，你的反应很可能会与多动症儿童或成人的惯常心理及身体行为类似。你会难以集中注意力，你的大脑可能会像在跑步机上的小松鼠一样：不停地跑，但就是到不了任何地方。坐着不动对你来说是一种折磨。父亲在产房外焦急不安地踱步，这是人们对成年人的刻板印象。我相信，ADD的过度活跃是由一股持续的、潜藏的焦虑情绪引发的。
If hyperactivity expresses anxiety, lethargy and underarousal express shame. Shame, like anxiety, is an attachment emotion. “Whenever someone becomes signiﬁcant to us, whenever another’s caring, respect or valuing matters, the possibility for generating shame emerges,” writes the psychologist Gershen Kaufman.2 e origin of shame is the feeling of having been cut oﬀ from the parent, of having lost the connection, if only momentarily. It cannot be helped, it occurs unavoidably as part of maturing. 如果过度活跃表现的是焦虑，那么昏昏欲睡和反应迟钝表现的就是羞愧。和焦虑一样，羞愧也是一种依恋情绪。心理学家格申·考夫曼（Gershen Kaufman）写道：“只要一个人对我们来说变得重要，只要别人的关心、尊重或认可很重要，产生羞愧的可能性就会出现。”2羞愧的起源是感觉自己被父母抛弃，感觉自己失去了与父母的联系，哪怕这种联系只是暂时的。这是不可避免的，是成熟过程中的一部分。
Shame becomes excessive if the parent’s signaling of disapproval is overly strong, or if the parent does not move to reestablish warm emotional contact with the child immediately—what Gershen Kaufman calls“restoring the interpersonal bridge.” Chronic stress experienced by the parent has the eﬀect of breaking that bridge. e small child does not have a large store of insight for interpreting the parent’s moods and facialexpressions: they either invite contact or forbid it. When the parent is distracted or withdrawn, the older infant or toddler experiences shame. Shame postures are observed in infants in response to nothing more than the parent breaking eye contact. e demeanor of the infants of depressed mothers is one of inactivity and the averted gaze. 如果父母表示不赞成的信号过于强烈，或者如果父母没有立即采取行动，重新与孩子建立温暖的情感联系——也就是格斯·考夫曼所说的“重新架起人际沟通的桥梁”——那么，羞耻感就会变得过度。父母长期承受的压力会破坏这种桥梁。年幼的孩子没有足够的洞察力来解读父母的情绪和面部表情：他们要么邀请接触，要么禁止接触。当父母心烦意乱或退缩时，年长一些的婴儿或幼儿就会感到羞耻。婴儿的羞耻姿态仅仅是因为父母中断了眼神接触而产生的。抑郁母亲的孩子的行为是消极的，会回避他人的目光。
Past the toddler phase, there will be many times the child’s behavior may trigger an angry response from the parent, the ADD child more than the average. Some parents are able to express anger without making the child feel cut oﬀ emotionally. ey convey disapproval without rejection. Otherparents, especially those with self-regulation problems of their own, may react with open or choked rage, punishing coldness or dejected withdrawal that signals defeat and disappointment. ese were the anger responses my children experienced from me. Each time this happens, shame is evoked in the child, especially as the parent usually believes—and makes the childbelieve—that whatever his (the parent’s) reaction is, the child is responsible for it. 在幼儿阶段之后，孩子的行为可能会多次引发父母的愤怒反应，多动症儿童比普通儿童更多。一些父母能够表达愤怒，而不会让孩子感到情感上的疏离。有些父母，尤其是那些自身存在自我调节问题的父母，可能会做出公开或隐忍的愤怒反应，以冷漠或沮丧的退缩来惩罚孩子，这表明他们感到挫败和失望。这些就是我的孩子从我这里感受到的愤怒反应。每次发生这种情况，孩子都会感到羞愧，特别是父母通常会认为——并让孩子也这样认为——无论他（父母）做出什么反应，都是孩子的错。
e deep feelings of shame associated with attention deﬁcit disorder are usually explained by the obvious fact that the ADD individual gets many things wrong. On the face of it, this makes sense. e adult or child with attention deﬁcit disorder may frequently oﬀend people or break a promise or be late somewhere. Given his inattentiveness and diﬃculties reading nonverbal social messages, he treads on toes—in both senses of that phrase. He carries memories of having failed at many tasks, of being deservedlycriticized—so he thinks—for many shortcomings. Such events, however,can only reinforce shame or provoke it—they cannot cause it. Its origins have nothing to do with bad deeds, failures or hurting anyone. Like its opposite number, hyperactivity, shame began as a normal physiological state that escaped regulation by the cortex. It becomes wound tightly into the self-identity of the individual. 与注意力缺陷障碍相关的深切的羞耻感通常可以用这样一个显而易见的事实来解释：患有注意力缺陷障碍的个体做错了很多事情。从表面上看，这是有道理的。患有注意力缺陷障碍的成人或儿童可能会经常冒犯他人、违背诺言或迟到。鉴于其注意力涣散和难以理解非言语社交信息，他会无意中冒犯他人——无论是字面意思还是引申义。他会记得自己在许多任务中失败，并认为自己理应因为许多缺点而受到批评。然而，这些事件只能强化羞耻感或激起羞耻感，而不会导致羞耻感。羞耻感的起源与不良
行为、失败或伤害任何人无关。与注意力缺陷障碍相反，羞耻感最初是一种不受大脑皮层控制的正常生理状态，它与个体自我认同紧密地交织在一起。John Ratey has aptly observed that “I’m sorry” is the most common phrase in the vocabulary of attention deﬁcit disorder. What strikes me immediately when I meet new ADD patients is how often they apologize. ey apologize when I ask them to speak louder, when they cannot easily answer a question, when I interrupt their ﬂow of speech to ask for moreinformation, when I tell them that we will wind up the session in a few minutes as time is running out. People ask forgiveness for being in myoﬃce in the ﬁrst place. eir opening words may be an apology: “I am sorry to be taking up your time. I am sure there are many people waiting to see you who need help a lot more than I do.” Of course, they also apologize if they think they have too serious a problem: “I’m sorry, I know it’sdiﬃcult to help me. You couldn’t have imagined you would be dealing with such a basket case.” ese expressions of remorse, where no oﬀense wascommitted, communicate a deep sense of shame. If the words did not betray shame, it could still be recognizable in the averted eyes. 约翰·瑞迪恰如其分地观察到，“对不起”是注意力缺陷障碍患者最常用的短语。当我遇到新的ADD患者时，最令我震惊的是他们道歉的频率。当我要求他们说话更大声时，他们道歉；当他们无法轻松回答问题时，他们道歉；当我打断他们的讲话以获取更多信息时，他们道歉；当我告诉他们时间不多了，我们几分钟后就要结束会谈
时，他们道歉。人们首先请求原谅的是占用我的办公室。他们的开场白可能是道歉：“占用您的时间，我很抱歉。”我确信有很多人在等着见你，他们需要的帮助远比我多。”当然，如果他们认为自己的问题太严重了，他们也会道歉：“对不起，我知道这很难帮助我。你无法想象你会处理这样一个烂摊子。”这些没有冒犯的悔恨表达传达了一种深深的羞耻感。如果这些话没有透露出羞耻感，那么在回避的目光中仍然可以认出羞耻感。
ADD swings back and forth between over-the-top, purposeless excitement and a nonrestful vegetative state in which the predominant emotion is shame. Some tend to get stuck at one or the other of these opposite poles.e two states may also be present at the same time, resulting in agitated,unfocused inactivity. ADD在过度、无目的的兴奋和不安的植物状态之间来回摇摆，植物状态的主要情绪是羞耻。有些人倾向于在这些相反的极点之一停滞不前。这两种状态也可能同时出现，导致不安、注意力不集中的无活动状态。
Like so much else about attention deﬁcit disorder, hyperactivity,lethargy and shame are closely connected with the neurological memories of the distant, stressed or distracted caregiver. ere will be a sense of discomfort as soon as the mind becomes aware of itself, because such awareness immediately triggers responses encoded with the infant’s distress at feeling emotionally alone. e mind then lapses into helpless lethargy, or races away, looking for something to attach to: some idea, some fantasy,some memory, conversation, music, reading—anything. When it cannot do so, there is intense unease—or the aversion to one’s own mind, which we call boredom. 与注意力缺陷障碍的许多其他症状一样，过度活跃、昏昏欲睡和羞耻感与照顾者遥远、紧张或心不在焉的神经记忆密切相关。一旦意识意识到自己，就会产生一种不适感，因为这种意识会立即引发与婴儿独自感受情感痛苦相关的编码反应。然后，意识就会陷入无助的昏睡，或者寻找一些东西来依附：一些想法、一些幻想、一些记忆、对话、音乐、阅读——任何东西。当它无法做到这一点时，就会产生强烈的不安——或者厌恶自己的意识，我们称之为无聊。
A requirement of healing, becoming whole, is circuitry in the brain that can carry diﬀerent messages and a diﬀerent, nonhelpless image of the self. ere is strong evidence that such circuits can develop at any time inlife, as can neural pathways to help the cortex to do its job of inhibition and regulation. 愈合、变得完整的一个要求是大脑中的回路，它可以传递不同的信息，并产生一个不同的、不无助的自我形象。有强有力的证据表明，这些回路可以在生命中的任何时候发展，就像帮助大脑皮层完成抑制和调节功能的神经通路一样。
It is not curing that ADD children need: they need to be helped to grow. What is required is not a change in parenting techniques but a change in parenting attitudes, based on a deeper understanding of the child. e adult with attention deﬁcit disorder needs also to gain a deeper understanding of herself, to undertake the task we will later describe as self-parenting.
治疗多动症儿童需要的不是治愈：他们需要被帮助成长。我们需要的不是改变育儿技巧，而是改变育儿态度，基于对孩子有更深入的了解。患有注意力缺陷障碍的成年人也需要对自己有更深入的了解，承担起我们将在后面描述的自我养育的任务。
We see this in attention deﬁcit disorder when the same child is able to attend to a subject in one type of environment but is unable to concentrate on the same topic in another. is situationality of ADD reﬂects the input of emotions, which play a powerful role in attention.
我们在注意力缺陷障碍中看到了这一点，当同一个孩子能够在一种环境下专注于一个主题，却无法在另一种环境下专注于同一个主题时。ADHD的情境性反映了情绪的输入，情绪在注意力中起着重要作用。
Integration of cognition withemotion—the melding of what we know with what we feel—is the very integration the healing process in ADD requires. Lack of it underlies the fragmentation of the ADD mind.
认知与情感的整合——我们所知道的事情与我们的感受的融合——正是ADD的治愈过程所需要的一体化。缺乏这种整合导致了ADD思维的碎片化。
understandable desire of parents is for point-by-point advice: What do I do in this situation? How do I handle that? Important as such questions are,they are secondary. e answers to them depend on how one interprets the child’s behaviors and on what the long-term objectives are. What we want to promote is not a mere change of behavior but a transformation of inner experience leading to the development of self-regulation. 父母希望得到逐条建议是可以理解的：在这种情况下我该怎么办？我该如何处理？虽然这些问题很重要，但它们是次要的。e对这些问题的回答取决于一个人如何解释孩子的行为以及长期目标是什么。我们想要促进的不仅仅是行为上的改变，而是内心体验的转变，从而导致自我调节的发展。
Every child with ADD has been wounded by a disruption in the 每个患有注意力缺陷多动症的孩子都受到了干扰的伤害。relationship between the caregiver and the sensitive infant. All the behaviors and mental patterns of attention deﬁcit disorder are external signs of the wound, or ineﬃcient defenses against feeling the pain of it. If development is to take place, energy has to be liberated for growth that now is consumed in protecting the self from further hurt. e key factor is cementing the attachment relationship. 照顾者与敏感婴儿之间的关系。注意力缺陷障碍的所有行为和心理模式都是创伤的外部迹象，或者是对感受创伤的无效防御。如果发展要发生，就必须释放能量以促进成长，而目前成长正消耗在保护自己免受进一步伤害上。关键因素是巩固依恋关系。
us, it is important to stimulate the portion of the brain that initiates emotional expression,” Dr.Cleeves Diamond writes. “Satisfying emotional needs is essential at anyage.”7
戴蒙德博士写道：“我们，重要的是要刺激大脑中引发情感表达的部分。在任何年龄，满足情感需求都是至关重要的。”7
In human brains, the circuitry of reason and emotion are closelyconnected, which is why troubled relationships lead directly to diﬃculties in brain processing. ey are not the only cause of disorganized thinking,but they are by far the commonest cause. Restoring relationships on a healthy basis promotes mental organization. New ways of processing emotions need new neural circuits, and the wiring of new circuits requires new experiences in a favorable emotional milieu. e relationship with the parents is the earth, the rain, the sun and the shade in which the child’s mental development must blossom
在人类的大脑中，理性与情感的回路是紧密相连的，这就是为什么混乱的人际关系会直接导致大脑处理困难。它们不是思维混乱的唯一原因，但它们是最常见的原因。在健康的基础上恢复人际关系会促进精神组织。处理情感的新方法需要新的神经回路，而新回路的布线需要在有利的情感环境中获得新的经验。与父母的关系是大地、雨水、阳光和阴影，孩子的精神发展必须在其中绽放。
In his book On Becoming a Person, Carl Rogers described a warm,caring attitude, for which he adopted the phrase unconditional positive regard because, he said, “It has no conditions of worth attached to it.” is is a caring, wrote Rogers, “which is not possessive, which demands no personal gratiﬁcation. It is an atmosphere which simply demonstrates Icare; not I care for you if you behave thus and so.”8 So the ﬁrst thing is to create some space in the child’s heart of hearts for the certainty that she is precisely the person the parents want and love. She does not have to doanything, or be any diﬀerent, to earn that love—in fact, she cannot doanything, because the love cannot be won and cannot be lost. It is not conditional. It is completely independent of the child’s behavior. It is justthere, regardless of which side the child is acting from, “good” or “bad.”e child can be ornery, unpleasant, whiny, uncooperative and plain rude,and the parent still lets her feel loved. Ways have to be found to let the child know that certain behaviors are unacceptable, without making the child herself feel not accepted. She has to be able to bring her unrest, her least likable side, to the parent without fear that it would threaten the relationship. When that is made possible, absolute security is established. We can reliably expect emotional growth to follow. 在《成为一个人》一书中，卡尔·罗杰斯描述了一种温暖、关爱的态度，他采用“无条件积极关注”这个短语来描述，因为，他说，“它没有附加任何有价值的条件。”罗杰斯写道，这是一种关爱，“不是占有性的，不要求个人的感激。它是一种
氛围，只是表现出我在乎你；不是因为你表现好我才在乎你。”因此，首先要在孩子的内心深处创造一些空间，让孩子确信自己正是父母所希望和爱戴的人。她不需要做任何事情，或者成为任何不同的人，就能赢得这份爱——事实上，她什么也不用做，因为这份爱不会被赢得，也不会被失去。它不是有条件的。它完全独立于孩子的行为。它就在那里，无论孩子是从“好”的一方还是从“坏”的一方表现出来。孩子可能会脾气暴躁、令人讨厌、发牢骚、不合作和无礼，而父母仍然让她感受到被爱。必须找到方法，让孩子知道某些行为是不可接受的，而不会让孩子自己感到不被接受。她必须能够向父母表达她的不安，她最不讨人喜欢的一面，而不用担心这会威胁到这段关系。当这成为可能时，绝对的安全感就建立了。我们可以可靠地期待情感上的成长。
Shame, as we have seen, is the physiological-emotional state resulting from the sense of being isolated, cut oﬀ. e ADD child is mired in shame. 正如我们所看到的，羞耻是一种生理-情感状态，是由被孤立、被切断的感觉引起的，ADD儿童陷入羞耻的泥潭。
As we have said, self-regulation is the goal of development; the lack of it is the fundamental impairment in ADD. One way of describing self-regulation is to say that it is the ability to maintain the internal environment within a functional and safe range, regardless of external circumstance. On the emotional level, the self-regulation of moods means that neither despondency nor uncontrolled exuberance, neither passive submission nor blind rage, control the mind. A person can experiencefrustration, disappointment or sadness without being seized by despair. Happiness does not need to be euphoria or anger hostility. Moods are not controlled by the vagaries of external events or by the moods of others. 正如我们所说，自我调节是发展的目标；缺乏自我调节是ADHD的根本损害。描述自我调节的一种方法是说，它是维持内部环境在功能和安全范围内的能力，而不管外部环境如何。在情绪层面上，情绪自我调节意味着既不沮丧，也不失控的兴奋，既不消极顺从，也不盲目愤怒，控制思想。一个人可以经历挫折、失望或悲伤，而不会被绝望所控制。幸福不一定是欣快症或愤怒敌意。情绪不受外部事件的反复无常或他人的情绪所控制。
Emotional self-regulation may be likened to a thermostat ensuring that the temperature in a home remains constant despite the extremes of weather conditions outside. When the environment becomes too cold, the heating system is switched on. If the air becomes overheated, the air conditioner begins to work. Or, to use an analogy from the animalkingdom, emotional self-regulation is like the capacity of the warm-blooded creature to exist in a greater range of environments. Its blood will neither chill nor overheat, no matter what the external temperature. e cold-blooded animal can endure a far narrower range of habitats because it does not have the capacity to self-regulate the internal environment. People with ADD, especially children, are like cold-blooded animals, in this sense.eir internal balance is too easily upset by even relatively slight external variations. ey are too often reacting automatically instead of acting purposefully. Many ﬁnd transitions stressful because they are not ﬂexible enough to adjust emotionally to even small changes. Others with ADD thrive on constant turmoil and change. is also is a failure of internal regulation expresed in the need to take up new activities, new relationships or new situations constantly because the interest and energy level cannot be sustained from within, without highly charged external stimuli. When circumstances make that impossible, there is chaos or emptiness. 情绪自我调节可以比作一个恒温器，确保家庭温度不受外界极端天气的影响而保持恒定。当环境变得过于寒冷时，就会打开供暖系统。如果空气变得过热，空调就会开始工作。或者，用动物界的类比来说，情绪自我调节就像温血动物在更大范围内生存的能力。无论外界温度如何，它的血液既不会变
冷，也不会变热。因为没有自我调节内部环境的能力，所以冷血动物能适应的生存环境范围小得多。从这个意义上说，有注意力缺陷多动症的人，尤其是儿童，就像冷血动物一样。eir内部平衡很容易被即使是相对轻微的外部变化所打乱。他们经常自动反应，而不是有目的地行动。许多人发现过渡期压力很大，因为他们不够灵活，无法在情感上适应哪怕是微小的变化。其他有注意力缺陷多动症的人则喜欢不断的动荡和变化。也是内部调节的失败，表现在需要不断从事新的活动、建立新的关系或面对新的情况，因为如果没有高度的外部刺激，就不能从内部维持兴趣和精力水平。当环境不允许这样做时，就会出现混乱或空虚。
Parents of an ADD child will often say that their son or daughter has a 多动症儿童的父母经常说，他们的儿子或女儿有“powerful” personality. Far from being powerful, the child is weak and vulnerable. It’s not his “power” but the ineﬃciency of the parents’ own emotional thermostat that enables the ﬂuctuations in the child’s moods to set the emotional tone of the whole household. A child in this situation is deeply insecure. He is made insecure by his lack of emotional self-control because there are no adults around able to maintain a steady and functioning environment, whatever his own particular internal states may be. ere is no development amid such a lack of safety. It only appears that the child’s moods are in control, because the parents are unaware of how profoundly their own mind states and their own lack of internal self-regulation aﬀect the child. It’s another vicious cycle. e parents need the child to be in balance only because they are not. But because the parents are not independently in balance themselves, the child cannot be. “强大”的人格。孩子远非强大，而是脆弱的。不是孩子的“力量”，而是父母自身情绪调节的无效性，使得孩子的情绪波动能够影响整个家庭的情绪基调。在这种情况下，孩子会深感不安全。由于缺乏情绪自我控制，他变得没有安全感，因为周围没有成年人能够维持一个稳定、正常的环境，无论他自己的内部状态如何。在如此缺乏安全的情况下，没
有发展可言。这似乎表明孩子的情绪是可控的，因为父母没有意识到他们自己的心理状态和缺乏自我调节对孩子产生了多么深远的影响。这是另一个恶性循环。父母需要孩子保持平衡，只是因为他们自己无法保持平衡。但由于父母自己无法独立地保持平衡，所以孩子也无法保持平衡。
e professional literature on ADD talks of “maintenance factors” that 关于ADD的专业文献讨论了“维持因素”，reinforce or trigger ADD traits. No maintenance factor is more powerful than the emotional states of the parents. Unresolved psychological conﬂicts between the parents, and within each parent individually, are a major source of unrest for the hypersensitive ADD child. is anxiety absorbed by sensitive children will lead to hyperactivity or other ADD-related behaviors. 加强或触发ADHD特征。没有比父母的情绪状态更强大的维护因素。父母之间以及父母个人之间的未解决的心理冲突是高度敏感的ADHD儿童不安定的主要来源。敏感儿童所吸收的焦虑会导致多动症或其他与ADHD相关的行为。
Self-regulation is intimately connected with a process developmental psychology has called individuation, or diﬀerentiation. Individuation—becoming a self-motivating, self-accepting person, a true individual—is the ultimate goal of development. As individuation unfolds, children are able to move more and more independently into the world, impelled by their own interests and needs. Less and less do they require that another person see exactly what they see in order to feel validated, or that another person feel exactly what they feel. ey may have needs and desire for closeness,warmth and mutual support with another human being, but they do not need to be emotionally fused with the other person—they can function on their own, if need be. 自我调节与一个发展心理学过程密切相关，该过程被称为个体化，或差异化。个体化——成为一个自我激励、自我接纳的人，一个真正的个体——是发展的最终目标。随着个体化的展开，孩子们能够越来越独立地进入这个世界，受到他们自己的兴趣和需求的驱使。他们越来越不需要让另一个人看到他们所看到的东西，以获得认可，或者让另一个人感受到他们所感受到的东西。他们可能有与另一个人亲近、温暖和相互支持的需要和愿望，但他们不需要与另一个人在情感上融合——如果有必要，他们可以独立发挥作用。
ey are likely to have unsatisfactory relationships with their partners, especially after children begin arriving to upset the fragile emotional balance between them. ey are also likely to fuse emotionally with one or another of their children. ere may be the semblance of a close relationship between parent and child, but in reality the child’s individuation is hindered, as he grows up feeling automatically responsible for the parent’s feeling states. Later the child will harbor a sense of responsibility for the whole world. Even what can be seen as selﬁsh behaviors represent nothing more than unconscious and desperate eﬀorts to throw oﬀ that sense of overwhelming responsibility.
他们与伴侣的关系可能不会令人满意，尤其是在孩子降生后，这会打破他们之间脆弱的情感平衡。他们还可能与一个或多个孩子在情感上融为一体。这可能是父母与孩子之间一种亲密关系的表象，但实际上，孩子的个异性受到了阻碍，他在成长过程中会自然而然地背负起父母的情感状态。之后，孩子会对整个世界产生一种责任感。甚至那些可以被视为自私的行为，也只不过是不自觉的、孤注一掷的摆脱那种压倒一切的责任感的努力。
ey learned to see their son as a separate individual rather than as an extension of themselves, no longer feeling personally devastated when he experienced distress or acted out in some immature fashion.
他们学会了将他们的儿子视为一个独立的人，而不是他们自己的延伸，当他们的儿子经历痛苦或以某种不成熟的方式行事时，他们不再感到个人受到毁灭性的打击。
当我们想到“懒惰”这个词时，我们意识到它不能解释任何事情。它只是对另一个不愿意做我们想让他们做的事情的人的负面判断。
懒惰和拖延并不是一个人的不变特征，而是他与世界的关系的表现，始于原籍家庭。
When we consider the word lazy, we realize that it does not explain anything. It is only a negative judgment made about another person who is unwilling to do what we want them to do.
So the laziness and the procrastination are not immutable traits of a person but expressions of his relationship with the world, beginning with the family of origin.
——费奥多尔·陀思妥耶夫斯基，《地下笔记》
And one may choose what is contrary to one’s own interests and sometimes one positively ought … One’s own free unfettered choice, one’s own caprice, however wild it may be, one’s own fancy worked up at times to frenzy … What man wants is simply independent choice, whatever that independence may cost and wherever it may lead. 一个人可以选择那些与自己利益相悖的事情，有时甚至确实应该这么做……自由无拘的抉择、任性的决定——无论这种任性多么疯狂、幻想多么狂热……人类所追求的，其实就是这种独立的决策权，无论这种独立会付出什么代价，无论它会将人引向何方。—FYODOR DOSTOEVSKY, “Notes from the Underground”
Counterwill is an automatic resistance put up by a human being with an incompletely developed sense of self, a reﬂexive and unthinking opposition to the will of the other. It is a natural but immature resistance arising from the fear of being controlled. Counterwill arises in anyone who has not yet developed a mature and conscious will of her own. Although it can remain active throughout life, normally it makes its most dramatic appearance during the toddler phase and again in adolescence. In manypeople, and in the vast majority of children with ADD, it becomes entrenched as an ever-present force and may remain powerfully active well into adulthood. It immensely complicates personal relationships, school performance and job or career success. “逆抗意志”是一种由自我意识尚未完全成熟的人所产生的自动抵抗行为，这种抵抗是对他人意志的一种无意识、本能性的反对。它源于对被控制的恐惧，因此是一种自然但尚不成熟的抵抗机制。任何尚未形成成熟、自觉的自我意志的人都会产生这种逆抗意志。虽然这种心理现象可能伴随人的一生，但它通常在幼儿期和青春期表现得最为明显。在许多人身上，尤其是在大多数患有多动症的孩子中，这种逆抗意志会逐渐固化为一股持续存在的力量，并可能一直活跃到成年期。它极大地扰乱了人际关系、学业表现以及职业发展。
Counterwill has many manifestations. e parent of a child with attention deﬁcit disorder will be familiar with them. Most obviously, it is expressed in verbal resistance—“I don’t have to,” “You can’t make me,” the constant arguing and countering whatever the parent proposes, the ubiquitous “You are not the boss of me.” Like a psychological immunesystem, counterwill functions to keep out anything that does not originate within the child herself. It is present when the four-year-old puts both hands over his ears to keep out the parent’s voice, or when the older child pins up an angry Keep Out sign on her door. It is visible in the body language of the adolescent and teenager: the sullen look and the shrugged shoulder. Its signs drive some adults around the bend, as in the futile “I’ll wipe that smirk oﬀ your face” of many a parent or teacher. Counterwill is also expressed through passivity. Every parent of an ADD child has had the experience of feeling intense frustration when, being pressured for time,they have tried to hurry their son or daughter along. e greater the parent’s anxiety and the greater the pressure he puts on the child, the more slothfully slow the child seems to become. Passivity begins to look like almost second nature to some of these children, although when she is highly motivated, the child will perform many tasks with alacrity. ispassivity, what people may call laziness, can signal a strong internal resistance. 反抗意志有多种表现形式。有注意力缺陷障碍孩子的父母应该对此非常熟悉。最明显的表现就是言语上的抗拒——“我不想做”，“你不能强迫我”；无论父母提出什么建议，孩子都会不断反驳；还有那句屡见不鲜的
“你不是我的老板”。就像一种心理上的“免疫系统”一样，反抗意志的作用就是排斥一切并非源自孩子自身的事物。当一个四岁的孩子用手捂住耳朵以屏蔽父母的声音时，或者当一个稍大一些的孩子在门上贴上“禁止入内”的标牌时，反抗意志就显现出来了。这种情绪可以从青少年的肢体语言中看出来：他们总是面带阴沉的表情，还会耸耸肩膀。这样的表现常常会让一些成年人失去耐心，比如许多家长或老师会试图“擦掉你脸上的那副得意的表情”，但这种努力往往是徒劳的。反抗心理也会通过消极被动的方式表现出来。任何有一个患有多动症孩子的家长都应该有过这样的经历：当时间紧迫时，他们试图催促自己的孩子加快动作，但却会感到无比沮丧。家长的焦虑程度越高，对孩子的压力就越大，而孩子表现得就越懒散、动作就越迟缓。对于这些孩子中的某些人来说，被动行为似乎已经成了他们的第二本能；不过当他们有强烈的动力时，也会迅速完成各种任务。这种被动性——也就是人们常说的懒惰——实际上可能暗示着他们内心存在着强烈的抗拒情绪。
Like all natural phenomena and all stages in the child’s life, counterwill has a positive purpose. It ﬁrst appears in the toddler to help in the task of individuating, of beginning to separate from the parent. In essence, the child erects a wall of no’s. Behind this wall, the child can gradually learn her likes and dislikes, aversions or preferences,without being overwhelmed by the far more powerful force generated by the parent’s will. Counterwill may be likened to the small fence one places around a tender young shoot to protect it from being eaten. e vulnerable little plant here is the child’s will. Without that protective fence, it cannot survive. In adolescence, counterwill serves the same goal, helping the young person loosen his psychological dependence on the family. It comes at a time when the sense of self is having to emerge out of the cocoon of the family. It is a defense mechanism to protect this fragile, threatened sense of self. By keeping out the parent’s expectations and demands,counterwill helps to make room for the growth of the child’s own self-generated motivations and preferences.
就像所有自然现象以及儿童成长过程中的各个阶段一样，“反抗意志”也有其积极的意义。这种意志最初出现在学步期的儿童身上，它的作用在于帮助儿童实现自我认同，开始与父母分离。实际上，儿童是通过建立起这层“拒绝的屏障”来逐渐了解自己的喜好与厌恶之处的。在这道屏障的保护下，儿童不必受到父母意志力的压制，从而能够自由地发展自己的意愿。这种反抗意志，就好比人们为脆弱的嫩芽搭建的小围栏，用来防止它们被吃掉一样。而这里所谓的“脆弱植物”，其实就是儿童的意志力；没有这道保护性的“围栏”，儿童的意志力就无法存活下去。在青春期，这种“反抗意志”发挥着同样的作用——帮助青少年摆脱对家庭的心理依赖。这一阶段，个体的自我意识正试图从家庭的束缚中逐渐萌芽出来。这种反抗意志实际上是一种防御机制，用来保护这种脆弱而容易受到威胁的自我意识。通过抵制父母的期望与要求，反抗意志为孩子自身产生的动力和偏好提供了成长空间。
underdevelopment. e weaker the child—or, for that matter, the adult—is psychologically, the more automatic and rigid the counterwill response becomes. A strong unconscious defense indicates a weak, undeveloped will,which is what is reﬂected in the oppositionality that seems intrinsic—but only seems that way—to the ADD personality. A strong defense is there only because there is threat, and the child is threatened only because a strong sense of his own self has not developed suﬃciently. So the root of the problem is that rather than being too powerful, the inner core of self, the true will, is stunted. is is why the various epithets such as stubborn,willful and so on indicate not a strong will but the lack of one. An emotionally self-conﬁdent person does not have to adopt an oppositional stance automatically. She may resist others’ attempts to control her, but she will not do so rigidly and defensively. If she opposes something, it is from a strong sense of what her true preferences are, not a knee-jerk reﬂex. A child not driven by counterwill does not automatically experience any advice,any expression of the parent’s opinion, as an attempt at control. Registering deep in her psyche is a sense of solidity about this inner core, this nucleus of the self, so there is no necessity to defend the will against being overwhelmed. I will be able to hang on to myself, an inner voice reassuresher, even if I listen to what somebody else thinks, or do what someone else wants me to do. I won’t lose my identity, so I don’t have to protect myself through resistance. I can aﬀord to cooperate. I can aﬀord to heed. Incontrast, the counterwill of the child with an underdeveloped self asserts itself ferociously.
发育不全。孩子——或者说任何成年人——在心理上越脆弱，其本能的抵抗反应就会变得越自动化、越僵化。这种强烈的无意识防御机制实际上反映了意志力的薄弱与不成熟；而这种对抗性似乎是注意力缺陷障碍人格的固有特征，但实际上只是表面现象罢了。这种强烈的防御机制的出现，只意味着存在威胁；而孩子之所以会感到受到威胁，正是因为他的自我意识尚未充分发展起来。因此，问题的根源并不在于内在自我的力量过于强大，而恰恰在于这种自我意识的发展受到了阻碍。正因如此，诸如“固执”、“任性”之类的称谓其实并不表示一个人拥有坚强的意志力，而恰恰说明他们缺乏意志力。一个在情感上充满自信的人并不会自动采取对抗的态度；她可能会抵制他人试图控制她的行为，但这种抵抗不会表现得过于僵化或防御性。如果她反对某件事，那是因为她清楚地知道自己真正的喜好是什么，而不是出于本能的反应。一个没有相反意志力驱使的孩子，也不会将父母的任何建议或意见视为控制自己的手段。深植于
她心灵深处的是一种对这一内在核心、自我本质的坚定信念。因此，没有必要为了抵御外界的影响而捍卫自己的意志。一个内在的声音安慰着她：即使我听从别人的意见、做别人希望我做的事，我依然能够保持自我。我不会失去自己的身份认同，也就无需通过抵抗来保护自己。我可以选择合作，也可以选择听从他人的建议。相比之下，那些自我意识尚未充分发展的孩子，他们的反抗意志就会表现得异常激烈。
For ADD children (and for ADD adults), it’s all or nothing. When anger arises, all feelings of attachment and love are banished. Since counterwill grows as attachment weakens, the child who is upset and angry may, in that moment, resist the parent with the emotional fury one would feel toward a despised enemy.
对于患有注意力缺陷障碍的儿童来说（以及成人也是如此），他们的情绪反应往往是非黑即白的。一旦愤怒情绪涌现，所有依恋和爱的感觉都会被彻底抛到一边。由于依恋关系的减弱会导致反抗意志的增强，因此那些感到沮丧和愤怒的儿童，在那一刻可能会以对可憎敌人那样的激烈情绪来抗拒父母。
ADD, all ADD children are unmotivated. eir absence of motivation is evident not only when it comes to activities and tasks expected of children their age but even in their approach to projects and plans that originally aroused their interest and enthusiasm. A lack of inner-directed purpose alsotypiﬁes a large number of ADD adults. ADD，所有ADD儿童都缺乏动机。他们的缺乏动机不仅表现在与他们年龄相符的活动和任务中，甚至表现在最初引起他们兴趣和热情的项目和计划中。缺乏内在导向的目的也是ADD成人的典型特征。
As with every aspect of the development of the ADD child, the growth of 正如ADD儿童发展的每个方面一样，大脑的生长true, internal motivation requires a secure attachment relationship with the parent. Without the safety of the attachment relationship, the small child will be too anxious to focus his attention on a meaningful exploration of the world around him.
的确，内部动机需要与父母建立安全依恋关系。如果没有这种安全依恋关系，年幼的孩子就会过于焦虑，无法将注意力集中在对他周围世界的有意义探索上。
True autonomy requires that the parents provide a supportive structure. 真正的自主性要求父母提供一个支持性的结构。
Without parental empathy, the child shuts down and hides behind the wall of emotional defenses; without structure, he becomes lost,uncertain and anxious
没有父母的同理心，孩子会封闭自己，躲在情感防御的墙后面；没有结构，他会迷失、不确定和焦虑。
Helping the child to label her feelings in words is what was referred to as symbolization when we discussed counterwill. It is also an important step in promoting autonomy. Words are symbols. ey stand for feelings and actions. Without the capacity to put things into symbols, children are driven to act out every strong feeling and every urge—it is the only way they can express themselves. ey are thus unable to take charge ofthemselves, impelled as they are to act by emotions they cannot identify. Without learning to symbolize emotions they are also likely to experience everything in terms of simple and opposing categories: people are alternately mean or nice, good or bad. It’s either “I love you, Mommy” or“I hate you.” e child has greater autonomy, a greater choice of possibleresponses, when she can say, “I didn’t like what Mrs. So-and-so said to me in class today,” than when she is restricted to “Mrs. So-and-so is mean.” Language supports freedom, including freedom from one’s own impulses. 当我们讨论反意志时，帮助孩子用语言表达自己的感受就是所谓的象征化。这也是促进自主性发展的重要一步。语言是符号，它们代表感受和行为。如果没有把事物符号化的能力，孩子们就会被迫表达自己强烈的感受和冲动——这是他们表达自己的唯一方式。因此，他们无法掌控自己，无法控制由他们无法识别的情绪驱使的行为。如果不学会把
情绪符号化，他们还可能用简单和对立的类别来体验一切：人们不是善就是恶，不是好就是坏。要么是“我爱你，妈妈”，要么是“我恨你”。当一个孩子有更大的自主权，有更多可能的回应选择，当她可以说“我不喜欢某某老师今天在课堂上对我说的话”，而不是只能局限于“某某老师很刻薄”时，她就获得了自由，包括从自己的冲动中获得自由。语言支持自由，包括从自己的冲动中获得自由。
e process of becoming connected with other people and learning appropriate humaninteractions, of developing into a social creature, is called socialization. Children don’t have to be trained into socialization. Because it is a fundamental human drive, we naturally develop connectedness and compassion if our own basic needs have been respected. Socialization is at the apex of a pyramid. e base is formed by secure attachment and autonomy. We often make the mistake with our children of puttingsocialization—the rules of social conduct, what is called “good behavior”—ahead of attachment and individuation. We try to make our kids act as truly socially responsible people at the expense of their emotional security and their autonomous sense of self. is may result in compliance, but not in the internal, organic growth of true morality and social responsibility. We can no more foster genuine socialization this way than we can balance a pyramid on its apex, upside down.
与他人建立联系并学习适当的人际交往，发展为社交生物的过程，称为社会化。孩子不需要被训练成社会化的人。因为这是一种基本的人类驱动力，如果我们自己的基本需求得到尊重，我们自然会发展出联系和同情心。社会化处于金字塔的顶端，其基础是安全依恋和自主性。我们经常犯的错误是，把社会化——社会行为规则，即所谓的“良好行为”——放在依恋和个体化之前。我们试图让我们的孩子在牺牲他们的情感安全和自主的自我意识的情况下，真正成为对社会负责任的人。这可能导致顺从，而不是真正的道德和社会责任的内在有机成长。我们不可能以这种方式培养真正的社会化，就像我们不可能把倒置的金字塔的尖端平衡起来一样。
“I love history and English,” Angus said, “but in my other subjects the teachers are always saying that Angus doesn’t pay attention. Doesn’t concentrate. Talks too much.’ I don’t take notes.” “我喜欢历史和英语，”安格斯说，“但在其他科目上，老师们总是批评我不认真听讲、无法集中注意力，还总爱说话太多。而且我从不做笔记。”“Why don’t you want to try medications?” I asked. “It’s just the way I am. It might sound strange, coming from an ex– drug addict, or however you want to phrase it—a clean addict now, a soberdrunk—but I don’t want to alter my mind with drugs as behavior control. I am the way I am as a result of my upbringing, the inﬂuence the world has had on me, what I have seen, my experiences. I am not about to try to change because some people say I’ve got a problem. And maybe I do.”“If you don’t think it’s a problem for you,” I said, “there is no reason you should take medications.”“It’s not that it’s not a problem,” Angus replied. “I can see how it is, but I don’t care. I’m not going to change myself in that way for others. I may be extremely selﬁsh in that respect, but I’m not going to put myself on pills so that I’m more—what’s the word—more controllable …” “你为什么不想尝试药物治疗呢？”我问道。 “这就是我的本性罢了。也许从一个曾经吸毒的人口中说出来会听起来有些奇怪——或者你可以用其他方式来形容我：一个已经戒毒的人，一个‘清醒的酒鬼’……但我并不想通过药物来控制自己的行为。我的这种性格，是成长环境、这个世界对我的影
响、我所见到的一切以及我的各种经历共同作用的结果。我不会仅仅因为别人说我有问题就试图去改变自己。或许，我确实有问题。” “如果你不认为这是个问题，”我说，“那么你就没有必要服用药物了。” “并不是说这不算个问题，”安格斯回答道。“我明白那种做法的道理，但我并不在乎。我不会为了别人而改变自己。在这方面，我或许确实很自私，但我绝不会服用那些药物来让自己变得更容易被控制……”“Manageable,” I suggested. “Yeah, that’s it, so that I am more manageable for my teachers. Iwouldn’t, ’cause that’s not me. If you can’t accept me for me, I think, then what are you looking for? What are you asking for from me?” I pressed the point a little: “But wouldn’t you like to be able to concentrate better, to be more focused in some of these other subjects besides just your favorite ones?” “这样会更容易被管理吧？”我提议道。 “对，就是这个意思——这样我的老师们就更容易对我进行管理了。但我可不愿意这样做，因为那不是真正的我。如果你无法接受真实的我，那么你究竟在追求什么？你到底想从我这里得到什么呢？” 我进一步强调道：“不过，你难道不希望自己在除了最喜欢的科目之外，也能更好地集中精力去学习其他科目吗？”“Nah,” said Angus. “I think ADD in itself isn’t that bad. “不，”安格斯说，“我认为多动症本身并不是什么坏事。问题在于……”put it on top of a broken home, or alcoholic parents, or bad crowds you get in with and you have trouble getting out of, you put it on top of not a lot of self-conﬁdence, then you get a serious problem. And then Ritalin isn’t going to take away everything. It’s just going to take away the ADD, not relieve all these other problems you have.” 如果再加上一个破裂的家庭、酗酒的父母，或是那些你很难摆脱的不良朋友圈子，再再加上缺乏自信，那么问题就严重了。而利他林并不能解决所有这些问题——它只能缓解注意力缺陷障碍的症状，却无法消除其他那些问题。
In the ADD child, low self-esteem is manifested not just by the self- 在多动症儿童中，低自尊不仅表现在自我-putdowns he may utter, such as “I’m dumb.” Above all, it is apparent in the perfectionism and in the dejection and discouragement he experiences when he fails at a task or loses in a game. Nor can he accept not being in the right. e fragile and self-rejecting ego is unable to endure any reminders of its fallibility. Many people with attention deﬁcit disorder retain that fragility into adulthood. 他可能会说出一些贬低自己的话，比如“我很笨”。最重要的是，当他在某项任务中失败或输掉比赛时，他明显会表现出完美主义和沮丧、气馁的情绪。他也无法接受自己不占理的情况。脆弱而自我否定的自我无法忍受任何关于其易错性的提醒。许多患有注意力缺陷障碍的人在成年后仍然保持着这种脆弱性。
e association between low self-esteem and attention deﬁcit disorder is not that the ﬁrst arises from the second,but that they both arise from the same sources: stress in the parenting environment and disrupted attunement/ attachment.
成人对注意力缺陷的深切羞耻感早于对糟糕成绩的回忆。低自尊和注意力缺陷障碍之间的联系不是前者源于后者，而是两者都源于相同的根源：养育环境中的压力和失调的协调/依恋。
Absolutely universal in the stories of all adults with ADD is the memory 在所有患有ADD的成人的故事中，绝对普遍的是记忆of never being comfortable about expressing their emotions. When asked whom they conﬁded in when as children they were lonely or in psychicpain, almost none recall feeling invited or safe enough to bare their souls to their parents. ey kept their deepest griefs to themselves. On the otherhand, many recall being hyperaware of the parents’ diﬃculties and struggles in the world, of not wanting to trouble them with their own petty and childish problems. e sensitive child, writes the Swiss psychotherapist Alice Miller, has “an amazing capacity to perceive and respond intuitively,that is unconsciously, to this need of the mother, or of both parents.”1 永远不习惯于表达自己的情感。当被问及他们小时候感到孤独或精神痛苦时向谁倾诉时，几乎没有人记得父母曾邀请他们或让他们感到足够安全，可以向他们袒露心声。他们把最深切的悲伤埋在心里。另一方面，很多人记得他们非常清楚父母在这个世界的困难与挣扎，不想用他们自己的琐碎和幼稚的问题来麻烦他们。瑞士心理治疗师爱丽丝·米勒写道，一个敏感的孩子“有惊人的直觉感知和反应能力，可以无意识地、本能地感知和回应母亲或双亲的需要。”
Since having a strong core self relies on acceptance of feelings, being out of touch with the emotional side puts a person out of touch with herself. What then remains to be esteemed? Only a false self, a concoction of what we would like to imagine ourselves to be and what we have divined others want us to be. Sooner or later, people come to realize that this false self—wanting what they think they should want, feeling what they think they should feel—does not work for them. When they look inside themselves, they discover a frightening emptiness, a vacuum, an absence of a true self or of intrinsic motivation. Many a time I have heard ADD adults say, “I don’t know who I am,” or, “I don’t know what I want to do in my life.”
既然拥有一个强大的核心自我取决于接受自己的感觉，那么与情感脱节就会让人与自我脱节。那么，还有什么值得尊重的呢？只有虚假的自我，也就是我们想要想象中的自我和我们推测别人希望我们成为的自我的混合体。人们迟早会意识到，这个虚假的自我——想要他们认为他们应该想要的东西，感受他们认为他们应该感受的情绪——对他们不起作用。当他们审视自己的内心时，他们发现了一种可怕的空虚，一种真空，一种真实自我或内在动机的缺失。我曾多次听到AD成人说，“我不知道我是谁”，或者“我不知道我想在我的一生中做什么”。
If the child does not feel accepted unconditionally, he learns to work for acceptance and attention. When he is not doing this work, he feelsanxious, owing to an unconscious fear of being cut oﬀ from the parent. 如果孩子没有无条件地感受到被接受，他就会学会为获得接受和关注而努力。当他不做这项工作时，他会感到焦虑，因为他无意识地害怕被父母抛弃。
Later—as an adult—when not doing something speciﬁc, he has a vagueuncase, the feeling that he should somehow be working. e adult has no psychological rest because the infant and child had never known psychological rest. He has a dread of rejection and an insatiable need to have his desirability and value aﬃrmed by others. Being wanted becomes a drug. Self-esteem is preempted by its false shadow, contingent self-esteem. What one does and what others think of it take precedence over who one is. 后来——作为一个成年人——当没有具体的事情可做时，他有一种模糊的不安全感，觉得自己应该做点什么。这个成年人没有心理上的休息，因为婴儿和儿童从未有过心理上的休息。他害怕被拒绝，并渴望自己的欲望和价值得到别人的确认。被需要成为了一种毒品。自尊被虚假的影子——偶然自尊——所取代。一个人做什么以及别人对此的看法优先于他是谁。
e stories of Elsa and David speak of something more painful than empathy and something less eﬀective, too: they speak of identiﬁcation. When a person empathizes,he can understand another’s feelings and even share them, but he is conscious of himself as a separate individual, capable of taking independent and useful action. When he becomes identiﬁed, that boundary disappears. He reacts as if he was himself the victim. He feels the victim’s humiliation,his helpless rage, his shame. is is not a state of adult human fellow feeling from which he can act eﬀectively: it is a state of memory. He is gripped by the past.
艾尔莎和大卫的故事讲述了一些比同理心更痛苦、更不有效的东西：他们讲述了认同感。当一个人产生同理心时，他可以理解他人的感受，甚至可以分享这些感受，但他意识到自己是一个独立的个体，能够采取独立和有用的行动。当他产生认同感时，这种界限就消失了。他的反应就好像自己是受害者一样。他感受到了受害者的羞辱、无助的愤怒和羞耻。这不是一种他可以从中采取有效行动的成年人的同理心状态：这是一种记忆状态。他被过去所束缚。
As the Harvard psychiatrist Judith Lewis Herman has pointed out, “To some degree everyone is a prisoner of the past.”1 Without knowing it, we often relive the past. What we take for present-day reality represents, in many situations, reactivated early memories stored in the implicit memorysystem, a vast and infallibly accurate record of past experiences. Implicit memory happens, according to the psychologist and memory researcher Daniel Schacter, “when people are inﬂuenced by a past experience without any awareness that they are remembering.”2 Unconscious emotions and conscious feelings, rapid shifts in mood and dramatic physiological changes in the body can occur under the impact of implicit memory. 正如哈佛精神病学家朱迪思·刘易斯·赫尔曼所指出的那样，“在某种程度上，每个人都是过去经历的囚徒。”1 我们常常在不经意间重温过去。在许多情况下，我们所认为的当下现实，代表的是存储于内隐记忆系统中的早期记忆被重新激活。内隐记忆是过去经历的庞大而准确无误的记录。根据心理学家、记忆研究专家丹尼尔·谢克特（Daniel Schacter）的说法，“当人们受到过去经历的影响，却丝毫没有意识到自己在回忆时，内隐记忆就发生了。”2 在内隐记忆的影响下，潜意识情感和意识情感、情绪的快速变化以及身体的剧烈生理变化都有可能发生。
if a sensitive child has unconscious or even preverbal experiences of feeling alone and cut oﬀ, misunderstood and shamed. From that arises a close identiﬁcation with the powerless, with the underdog—the people Dostoevsky called “the insulted and the injured.”
